## GHSA-pcrw-h46h-c93h · mongoose

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→NOSQL`

### Root cause

The `_countDocuments` and `_count` methods in Mongoose queries did not properly sanitize user-supplied filter conditions. Specifically, they were missing a call to `_castConditions()` which includes the `sanitizeFilter` logic, allowing malicious operators like `$where` to be passed directly to the database.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass Mongoose's query sanitization, potentially executing arbitrary JavaScript code via `$where` or other dangerous operators, leading to data exfiltration, modification, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/query.js
+++ b/lib/query.js
@@ -2625,11 +2625,7 @@ Query.prototype.findOne = function(conditions, projection, options) {
  */
 
 Query.prototype._count = async function _count() {
-  try {
-    this.cast(this.model);
-  } catch (err) {
-    this.error(err);
-  }
+  this._castConditions();
```

### Fix summary

The patch ensures that `_count`, `_countDocuments`, and `cursor` methods explicitly call `this._castConditions()`. This method applies the necessary sanitization and casting to the query conditions, preventing malicious operators from being passed to the database.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 6 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Python, JavaScript
> → GHSA-jfwg-rxf3-p7r9
> → GHSA-grp3-h8m8-45p7
> → GHSA-98xf-r82g-9mhx
> → GHSA-7c7c-373r-gfjj
> → GHSA-gg4r-jv3p-qx3c

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/Automattic/mongoose/commit/7e912af3fac52cb0eea4cd71e24d65318916831a)

## GHSA-xggf-m53x-jc3f · node-opcua-server

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insecure default behavior in how namespace-level Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) permissions were handled. Specifically, the `DefaultRolePermissions` property, when not explicitly set, would default to `null` or `undefined` instead of an empty array or a properly inherited policy. This 'unbound' state caused the permission lookup logic to short-circuit and incorrectly grant anonymous users write and call access to OPC UA nodes within affected namespaces, bypassing intended restrictions.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could bypass namespace-level RBAC, gaining unauthorized write and call access to OPC UA nodes. This could lead to data manipulation, unauthorized command execution, or disruption of industrial control processes if the server is used in an operational technology (OT) environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/node-opcua-address-space/source/session_context.ts
+++ b/packages/node-opcua-address-space/source/session_context.ts
@@ -262,5 +262,13 @@
             }
         }
     }
-    return null;
+    // Fall back to the policy configured programmatically via Namespace.setDefaultRolePermissions().
+    // The NamespaceMetadata node is only bound to that field when setNamespaceMetaData() is called
+    // (which throws on NodeSet2-loaded namespaces), and is absent entirely for a programmatically
+    // created namespace. Consulting the field here keeps the configured default consistent between
+    // the local getter (BaseNode.getRolePermissions(inherited=true)) and this enforcement path,
+    // so setDefaultRolePermissions() takes effect without also requiring setNamespaceMetaData().
+    return namespace.getDefaultRolePermissions();
 }
 
 /**
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the issue by ensuring that `DefaultRolePermissions` and `DefaultUserRolePermissions` are properly initialized. It modifies the permission lookup logic to correctly fall back to programmatically configured namespace policies when the metadata nodes are unbound. Additionally, it initializes these permission properties with a 'Good, Null' value if they are unset, preventing the short-circuiting behavior that led to the bypass.

## GHSA-2h4w-29cv-2cmv · autogen

2026-11-10 · Python · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete shell operator filter in 'restricted mode'. While some shell metacharacters were blocked, newline characters were not. This allowed an attacker to inject newlines into the command string, effectively bypassing the `allowed` and `readonly` lists and executing arbitrary commands after the initially allowed command.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass security restrictions (e.g., `readonly=True` or explicit `allowed` command lists) and execute arbitrary commands on the system where the sandbox shell adapter is running, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ag2/tools/sandbox/filter.py
+++ b/ag2/tools/sandbox/filter.py
@@ -40,7 +40,7 @@
 # Shell syntax a model may try in restricted mode. Restricted mode runs the
 # parsed argv without a shell, so none of it takes effect; rejecting it up
 # front gives the model a clear error instead of a confusing one from the
-# command (``cat: '>': No such file``).
-_SHELL_OPERATORS: tuple[str, ...] = (">", ">>", "|", ";", "&&", "||", "`", "$(")
+# command (``cat: '>': No such file``).
+_SHELL_OPERATORS: tuple[str, ...] = (">", ">>", "<", "|", ";", "&", "&&", "||", "\n", "\r", "`", "$(")
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds newline characters (\n, \r) to the list of shell operators that are explicitly blocked in restricted mode. It also refactors how commands are processed, ensuring that in restricted mode, commands are split into `argv` using `shlex.split` and executed directly without `sh -c`, preventing shell expansion of metacharacters after the initial check.

## GHSA-v924-7rp8-4mxh · activestorage

2026-11-10 · Ruby · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from Active Storage creating temporary files for blob processing (e.g., video previews, image analysis) using the original filename's extension. Certain extensions, like '.avs' (AviSynth scripts), could be interpreted as executable code by tools like FFmpeg, leading to arbitrary command execution. Additionally, other extensions could lead to arbitrary file reads.

### Impact

An attacker could upload a specially crafted file with a malicious extension, leading to remote code execution on the server or arbitrary file disclosure, compromising the integrity and confidentiality of the system.

### Key diff

```
-	      name: [ "ActiveStorage-#{id}-", filename.extension_with_delimiter ],
+	      name: [ "ActiveStorage-#{id}-", tempfile_extension ],
 
 private
+    # The extension is there for tools that cannot tell the type from the bytes (316d87e4: ImageMagick
+    # needs it for an SVG with no XML declaration), but some tools choose a decoder on it alone: FFmpeg
+    # runs a `.avs` file as an AviSynth script. So the uploaded extension is kept only when it denotes
+    # the blob's media type.
+    def tempfile_extension
+      extension = filename.extension_with_delimiter
+      return "" if extension.blank?
+
+      type_for_extension = Marcel::MimeType.for(name: filename.to_s)
+      return "" if type_for_extension == Marcel::MimeType::BINARY || type_for_extension != media_type
+
+      extension
+    end
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies how temporary file extensions are handled. It now only preserves the original filename's extension if it accurately reflects the blob's content type. Otherwise, the temporary file is created without an extension, preventing tools from misinterpreting the file based on a potentially malicious extension.

## GHSA-xwgp-w3pj-q5h3 · @ohif/extension-dicom-pdf

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application was vulnerable to DOM-based XSS because it did not properly validate or sanitize the MIME type of encapsulated documents. An attacker could manipulate the `MIMETypeOfEncapsulatedDocument` field to inject malicious HTML or JavaScript, which would then be rendered by the browser without proper sandboxing or content verification.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary JavaScript in the context of the user's browser, leading to session hijacking, data theft, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- /dev/null
+++ b/extensions/dicom-pdf/src/utils/displayableDocumentTypes.ts
@@ -0,0 +1,170 @@
+export const DISPLAYABLE_DOCUMENT_TYPES: Record<string, DisplayableDocumentType> = {
+  'application/pdf': {
+    mimeType: 'application/pdf',
+    strategy: 'object',
+    signature: [0x25, 0x50, 0x44, 0x46, 0x2d], // "%PDF-"
+    signatureSearchLimit: PDF_SIGNATURE_SEARCH_LIMIT,
+  },
+  'text/html': {
+    mimeType: 'text/html',
+    strategy: 'iframe',
+    sandbox: '',
+  },
+  // ... other types with specific strategies and sandboxing
+};
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a strict allowlist for displayable document MIME types and implements signature checks for certain types (like PDF) to ensure the content matches the declared type. HTML content is now rendered within a sandboxed iframe to prevent script execution, and a `normalizeDocumentMimeType` function cleans up raw MIME type strings.

## GHSA-5v64-555j-wjxm · rack

2026-11-10 · Ruby · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the Rack multipart parser's internal buffer (`@sbuf`) was not properly reset or truncated after processing parts of the input stream. This allowed the buffer to grow indefinitely when handling specially crafted multipart requests, leading to excessive memory consumption.

### Impact

An attacker could send a malicious multipart request, causing the server to consume an unbounded amount of memory and eventually crash or become unresponsive, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/rack/multipart/parser.rb
+++ b/lib/rack/multipart/parser.rb
@@ -494,6 +494,7 @@ def handle_mime_head
           # buffering. Note that the actual limit is the higher of 64KB and the buffer size (1MB by default)
           raise Error, "multipart mime part header too large" if @sbuf.rest.bytesize > MIME_HEADER_BYTESIZE_LIMIT
 
+          @sbuf.string = @sbuf.rest
           return :want_read
         end
       end
@@ -514,8 +515,8 @@ def handle_mime_body
             update_retained_size(body.bytesize) if @body_retained
             @collector.on_mime_body @mime_index, body
             @sbuf.pos += delta
-            @sbuf.string = @sbuf.rest
           end
+          @sbuf.string = @sbuf.rest
           :want_read
         end
       end
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by explicitly reassigning `@sbuf.string` to `@sbuf.rest` after processing each part of the multipart data. This ensures that the buffer is truncated to only contain the unprocessed remaining data, preventing unbounded growth.

## GHSA-6qrw-m8cv-hcxf · node-forge

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signature verification process did not strictly enforce canonical DER encoding for the DigestInfo structure. The BER parser and validator were too lenient, tolerating non-canonical elements such as extra elements, NULL parameters with content, non-minimal lengths, and non-canonical OIDs. This laxity allowed an attacker to craft a non-canonical DigestInfo that, when combined with a low exponent RSA key, could lead to a signature forgery.

### Impact

An attacker could forge RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signatures, potentially allowing them to bypass authentication, validate malicious data, or impersonate legitimate entities if the signatures were used for integrity or authenticity checks.

### Key diff

```
-          // compare the given digest to the decrypted one
-          return digest === capture.digest;
+          // require a canonical DER DigestInfo (RFC 8017 8.2.2). The BER
+          // parser and validator above tolerate extra elements, NULL
+          // parameters with content, non-minimal lengths, and non-canonical
+          // OIDs, all of which leave room for low exponent signature forgery.
+          // Re-encode with and without NULL parameters and compare bytes.
+          var withParameters = _encodeDigestInfo(oid, capture.digest, true);
+          var withoutParameters =
+            _encodeDigestInfo(oid, capture.digest, false);
+          if(d.substring(0, withParameters.length) !== withParameters &&
+            d.substring(0, withoutParameters.length) !== withoutParameters) {
+            throw new Error(
+              'ASN.1 object does not contain a valid RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 ' +
+              'DigestInfo value.');
+          }
+          // compare the given digest to the decrypted one
+          return digest === capture.digest;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new internal function `_encodeDigestInfo` to canonically DER encode a DigestInfo structure. During signature verification, after parsing the DigestInfo, the patch re-encodes the captured digest and OID using this new function, both with and without NULL parameters. It then strictly compares the decrypted DigestInfo bytes against these canonical encodings, rejecting any signature that does not match a canonical form.

## GHSA-gg4r-jv3p-qx3c · mongoose

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→NOSQL`

### Root cause

The Mongoose library failed to correctly apply the `sanitizeFilter` option when it was configured at the connection level. This meant that even if a developer intended to sanitize user input in query conditions, the sanitization logic was not executed, leaving the application vulnerable to NoSQL injection.

### Impact

An attacker could craft malicious input to bypass intended query logic, potentially leading to unauthorized data access, modification, or deletion, depending on the application's use of the vulnerable queries.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/query.js
+++ b/lib/query.js
@@ -2405,14 +2405,10 @@ Query.prototype.mongooseOptions = function(v) {
  */
 
 Query.prototype._castConditions = function() {
-  let sanitizeFilterOpt = undefined;
-  if (this.model?.db.options?.sanitizeFilter != null) {
-    sanitizeFilterOpt = this.model.db.options.sanitizeFilter;
-  } else if (this.model?.base.options?.sanitizeFilter != null) {
-    sanitizeFilterOpt = this.model.base.options.sanitizeFilter;
-  } else {
-    sanitizeFilterOpt = this._mongooseOptions.sanitizeFilter;
-  }
+  const sanitizeFilterOpt = this.model?.db.config?.sanitizeFilter ??
+    this.model?.db.options?.sanitizeFilter ??
+    this.model?.base.options.sanitizeFilter ??
+    this._mongooseOptions.sanitizeFilter;
 
   if (sanitizeFilterOpt) {
     sanitizeFilter(this._conditions);
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `_castConditions` and `cast` methods to correctly retrieve the `sanitizeFilter` option. It now prioritizes `model.db.config.sanitizeFilter`, then `model.db.options.sanitizeFilter`, then `model.base.options.sanitizeFilter`, and finally `_mongooseOptions.sanitizeFilter`, ensuring the connection-level setting is applied.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 6 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Python, JavaScript
> → GHSA-jfwg-rxf3-p7r9
> → GHSA-grp3-h8m8-45p7
> → GHSA-98xf-r82g-9mhx
> → GHSA-7c7c-373r-gfjj
> → GHSA-pcrw-h46h-c93h

## GHSA-rhr8-3pm8-cq29 · node-forge

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the ASN.1 decoder in node-forge did not properly limit the recursion depth when parsing BIT STRING values. An attacker could craft a malicious ASN.1 structure with deeply nested BIT STRINGs, causing the parser to recurse excessively.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted ASN.1 input that would lead to a stack overflow or excessive memory consumption, causing the application to crash and resulting in a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/asn1.js
+++ b/lib/asn1.js
@@ -600,7 +600,8 @@ function _fromDer(bytes, remaining, depth, options) {
         var subOptions = {
           // enforce strict mode to avoid parsing ASN.1 from plain data
           strict: true,
-          decodeBitStrings: true
+          decodeBitStrings: true,
+          maxDepth: options.maxDepth
         };
         var composed = _fromDer(bytes, remaining, depth + 1, subOptions);
         var used = start - bytes.length();
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by passing the `maxDepth` option to recursive calls of the `_fromDer` function. This ensures that the recursion depth is properly limited, preventing excessive nesting and resource exhaustion when decoding ASN.1 structures.

---

## GHSA-xxrh-5hgg-gj4h · @nuxt/content

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application's SQL query sanitization logic, specifically for the WHERE clause, did not adequately prevent the injection of SQLite function calls. While it stripped string literals, it failed to identify and block function-like syntax (e.g., `randomblob()`, `zeroblob()`) that could be used to execute resource-intensive operations within the database.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could inject SQLite functions into the `WHERE` clause of content queries, leading to the execution of computationally expensive operations. This could exhaust server resources, causing a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/runtime/internal/security.ts
+++ b/src/runtime/internal/security.ts
@@ -1,6 +1,11 @@
 const SQL_COMMANDS = /SELECT|INSERT|UPDATE|DELETE|DROP|ALTER|\$/i
 const SQL_COUNT_REGEX = /^COUNT\((DISTINCT )?([a-z_]\w+|\*)\) as count$/i
 const SQL_SELECT_REGEX = /^SELECT (.*) FROM (\w+)( WHERE .*)? ORDER BY ([\"\w,\s]+) (ASC|DESC)( LIMIT \d+)?( OFFSET \d+)?$/
+// Parentheses in WHERE are only valid after these keywords (grouping / IN lists).
+// Anything else that looks like a call (name(, "name"(, [name](, `name`() is disallowed.
+const SQL_WHERE_PAREN_KEYWORDS = /\b(?:WHERE|AND|OR|IN)\s*\(/gi
+// Bare identifiers use a word boundary; quoted/bracketed forms match the whole identifier unit.
+const SQL_FUNCTION_CALL = /(?:\b[A-Z_]\w*|["`][A-Z_]\w*["`\]])\s*\(/i
 
 /**
  * Assert that the query is safe
@@ -60,6 +65,15 @@ export function assertSafeQuery(sql: string, collection: string) {
     if (noString.match(SQL_COMMANDS)) {
       throw new Error('Invalid query: WHERE clause contains unsafe SQL commands')
     }
+    // Block SQLite function calls (randomblob, zeroblob, hex, length, …),
+    // including quoted/bracketed forms SQLite accepts as identifiers: "abs"(, [abs](, `abs`(.
+    // Only single-quoted value literals are stripped so identifier quotes stay visible to the matcher.
+    // The query builder never emits functions in WHERE; only grouping and IN (...).
+    const noSingleQuoted = cleanupQuery(where, { removeSingleQuoted: true })
+    const withoutGroupingParens = noSingleQuoted.replace(SQL_WHERE_PAREN_KEYWORDS, ' ')
+    if (SQL_FUNCTION_CALL.test(withoutGroupingParens)) {
+      throw new Error('Invalid query: WHERE clause contains unsafe SQL expressions')
+    }
   }
 
   // ORDER BY
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces new regular expressions (`SQL_WHERE_PAREN_KEYWORDS`, `SQL_FUNCTION_CALL`) and modifies the `cleanupQuery` function to more robustly identify and block SQLite function calls within the `WHERE` clause. It specifically targets function-like syntax, including quoted/bracketed forms, to prevent their execution.

## GHSA-2gff-2mmg-m73p · actionpack

2026-11-10 · Ruby · **HIGH** 7.4 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The `RemoteIp` middleware in Action Pack did not adequately handle conflicting IP-related headers, specifically when both `Forwarded` and `X-Forwarded-For` headers were present. An attacker could manipulate these headers to spoof their IP address, especially if the application was behind certain proxies (like AWS ELB) that might set headers in a confusing way or if the `ip_spoofing_check` was disabled.

### Impact

An attacker could spoof their IP address, potentially bypassing IP-based access controls, logging incorrect IP addresses for malicious activities, or interfering with features that rely on accurate client IP identification.

### Key diff

```
-        should_check_ip = @check_ip && client_ips.last && forwarded_ips.last
-        if should_check_ip && !forwarded_ips.include?(client_ips.last)
-          # We don't know which came from the proxy, and which from the user
-          raise IpSpoofAttackError, "IP spoofing attack?! " \
-            "HTTP_CLIENT_IP=#{@req.client_ip.inspect} " \
-            "HTTP_X_FORWARDED_FOR=#{@req.x_forwarded_for.inspect}" \
-            " HTTP_FORWARDED=" + @req.forwarded_for.map { "for=#{_1}" }.join(", ").inspect if @req.forwarded_for.any?
-        end
+        check_ip(client_ips, forwarded_ips) if @check_ip
...
+      def check_ip(client_ips, forwarded_ips)
+        if client_ips.last && forwarded_ips.last && !forwarded_ips.include?(client_ips.last)
+          # We don't know which came from the proxy, and which from the user
+          raise_spoofing_error
+        end
+
+        if @req.get_header("HTTP_FORWARDED") && @req.x_forwarded_for
+          x_forwarded_ips = XForwardedForParser.new("HTTP_X_FORWARDED_FOR" => @req.x_forwarded_for).forwarded_for || []
+          x_forwarded_client = filter_proxies(sanitize_ips(x_forwarded_ips).reverse!).first
+          forwarded_client = filter_proxies(forwarded_ips).first
+
+          # Compare strings, not parsed addresses, so the client cannot choose how its
+          # own address is spelled.
+          if x_forwarded_client && x_forwarded_client != forwarded_client
+            raise_spoofing_error
+          end
+        end
+      end
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a more robust `check_ip` method that explicitly compares the client IP derived from `X-Forwarded-For` with the client IP derived from the `Forwarded` header. If these conflict, an `IpSpoofAttackError` is raised, preventing the spoofed IP from being used. It also updates documentation to reflect the `Forwarded` header's role.

## GHSA-5m6c-38r8-5w76 · github.com/dagu-org/dagu/v2

2026-11-10 · Go · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application allowed DAG file paths to be specified with relative paths, including '.' which could be resolved outside the intended base directory. Additionally, the `locateDAG` function did not properly sanitize or restrict absolute paths or paths containing directory separators, allowing an attacker to bypass path containment and workspace isolation.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary DAG specification files from the file system, potentially leading to information disclosure or the ability to execute unauthorized DAGs if the content of these files could be manipulated.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/persis/file/dag/store.go
+++ b/internal/persis/file/dag/store.go
@@ -108,10 +108,10 @@ func New(baseDir string, opts ...Option) exec.DAGStore {
 	if options.FlagsBaseDir == "" {
 		options.FlagsBaseDir = filepath.Join(baseDir, "flags")
 	}
-	// Build search paths in deterministic order: baseDir first, then ".", then additional paths.
+	// Build search paths in deterministic order: baseDir first, then additional paths.
 	seen := make(map[string]struct{})
 	var searchPaths []string
-	for _, p := range append([]string{baseDir, "."}, options.SearchPaths...) {
+	for _, p := range append([]string{baseDir}, options.SearchPaths...) {
 		if _, ok := seen[p]; ok {
 			continue
 		}
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes '.' from the default search paths and modifies the `locateDAG` function to use `fileutil.ResolveExistingPathWithinBase`. This new utility ensures that any resolved path remains strictly within the configured base directory, preventing path traversal. It also explicitly handles absolute paths by attempting to make them relative to the base directory.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 105 occurrences**

## GHSA-ghwv-vrp2-2975 · github.com/ultravioletrs/cocos

2026-11-10 · Go · **HIGH** 6.3 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The vulnerability arises from a Time-of-Check to Time-of-Use (TOCTOU) race condition. The `MarkContextUsed` function, which marks a certificate_request_context as used to prevent reuse, was called after the cryptographic operations (signing and MAC calculation) were performed. This allowed an attacker to potentially reuse a context that was checked as unused but then used before being marked.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially reuse a `certificate_request_context` in a TLS 1.3 handshake, leading to a bypass of the intended security properties of the attested TLS implementation. This could compromise the integrity or authenticity of the handshake.

### Key diff

```
Before:
```go
	// ... cryptographic operations ...
	if err := session.MarkContextUsed(ctx); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return append(append(certBytes, cvBytes...), finBytes...), nil
```

After:
```go
	if err := session.MarkContextUsed(ctx); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	// ... cryptographic operations ...
	return append(append(certBytes, cvBytes...), finBytes...), nil
```

### Fix summary

The patch moves the `session.MarkContextUsed(ctx)` call to occur immediately after the `ctx` is determined and before any cryptographic operations or message marshaling that depend on its uniqueness. This ensures that the context is marked as used as early as possible, preventing TOCTOU issues.

## GHSA-752m-7p75-49gf · altair-fastify-plugin

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application previously initialized the iframe sandbox using URL parameters, specifically `plugin_sandbox_opts`. This allowed an attacker to control the `scriptUrls` and `styleUrls` provided in the URL, which were then directly injected into the iframe's DOM without proper validation or sanitization.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary JavaScript into the iframe sandbox, leading to DOM-based Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). This could allow them to execute malicious scripts in the context of the sandbox, potentially leading to data theft or other malicious actions within the sandboxed environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/altair-iframe-sandbox/src/plugin-sandbox.ts
+++ b/packages/altair-iframe-sandbox/src/plugin-sandbox.ts
-  const searchParams = new URLSearchParams(window.location.search);
-  const pluginSandboxOptsStr = searchParams.get('plugin_sandbox_opts');
-  const pluginSandboxOpts = pluginSandboxOptsStr
-    ? (JSON.parse(pluginSandboxOptsStr) as PluginParentWorkerOptions)
-    : undefined;
-  if (!pluginSandboxOpts) {
-    throw new Error('Invalid plugin options provided!');
-  }
-  if (pluginSandboxOpts.type !== 'scripts') {
-    throw new Error('Invalid plugin option type provided!');
-  }
-  if (pluginSandboxOpts.styleUrls) {
-    for (const style of pluginSandboxOpts.styleUrls) {
-      await injectStylesheet(style);
-    }
-  }
-  if (pluginSandboxOpts.scriptUrls) {
-    for (const script of pluginSandboxOpts.scriptUrls) {
-      await injectScript(script);
-    }
+  const initialization =
+    getDeprecatedQueryInitialization() ?? (await getMessageInitialization());
+  window.__ALTAIR_PLUGIN_PARAMS__ = initialization.params;
+  for (const style of initialization.styleUrls) {
+    await injectStylesheet(style);
+  }
+  for (const script of initialization.scriptUrls) {
+    await injectScript(script);
   }
```

### Fix summary

The patch transitions the iframe sandbox initialization from using URL parameters to a more secure `postMessage` mechanism. It introduces a `getMessageInitialization` function that listens for `PLUGIN_SANDBOX_INITIALIZE` messages from the parent window, validating the origin and payload before injecting scripts and styles. The deprecated query-based initialization is now only allowed for a specific hosted sandbox origin for backward compatibility.

## GHSA-358w-w75h-x6rx · github.com/gopacket/gopacket

2026-11-10 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The gopacket library's SCTP chunk sub-decoders did not sufficiently validate the lengths of incoming SCTP parameters and chunk data. Specifically, the `decodeSCTPParameter` function and various chunk decoders (Init, Sack, Heartbeat, Error, Shutdown) would trust attacker-controlled length fields without bounds checking, leading to out-of-bounds reads or panics when processing malformed SCTP segments.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious SCTP segment that, when parsed by the gopacket library, would cause the application to panic and crash, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/layers/sctp.go
+++ b/layers/sctp.go
@@ -190,14 +190,34 @@ type SCTPParameter struct {
 	Value        []byte
 }
 
-func decodeSCTPParameter(data []byte) SCTPParameter {
+// Minimum lengths covering the fixed fields each SCTP decoder reads.
+const (
+	sctpParameterMinLength = 4
+	sctpInitMinLength      = 20
+	sctpSackMinLength      = 16
+	sctpShutdownMinLength  = 8
+)
+
+func decodeSCTPParameter(data []byte) (SCTPParameter, error) {
+	if len(data) < sctpParameterMinLength {
+		return SCTPParameter{}, errors.New("invalid SCTP parameter length")
+	}
 	length := binary.BigEndian.Uint16(data[2:4])
+	if length < sctpParameterMinLength || int(length) > len(data) {
+		return SCTPParameter{}, errors.New("invalid SCTP parameter length")
+	}
 	return SCTPParameter{
 		Type:         binary.BigEndian.Uint16(data[0:2]),
 		Length:       length,
 		Value:        data[4:length],
 		ActualLength: roundUpToNearest4(int(length)),
-	}
+	}, nil
+}
+
+// nextSCTPParameter skips past p. The last parameter's padding may lie
+// outside the chunk length (RFC 9260 3.2), so the skip is clamped.
+func nextSCTPParameter(data []byte, p SCTPParameter) []byte {
+	return data[min(p.ActualLength, len(data)):]
 }
 
 func (p SCTPParameter) Bytes() []byte {
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds robust length validation checks to `decodeSCTPParameter` and other SCTP chunk decoders. It introduces minimum length constants for various SCTP structures and explicitly checks if reported lengths or parameter counts exceed the actual available data, returning an error instead of proceeding with an out-of-bounds access.

## GHSA-wvg4-6p58-3gh3 · rack

2026-11-10 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The `Rack::Static` component did not properly normalize dot-segments (e.g., `../`) in paths before applying header rules. This mismatch between how the path was processed for header rules versus how it was served allowed an attacker to bypass security restrictions.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a URL using dot-segments to bypass intended header rules, potentially leading to the exposure of sensitive headers or other unintended behavior for static files.

### Key diff

```
-      path = ::Rack::Utils.unescape_path(path)
+      path = ::Rack::Utils.clean_path_info(::Rack::Utils.unescape_path(path))
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a call to `::Rack::Utils.clean_path_info` after unescaping the path in the `applicable_rules` method. This ensures that dot-segments are normalized consistently, preventing attackers from bypassing header rules.

## GHSA-2j8w-xcrc-x985 · rack

2026-11-10 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 4.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The previous fix for CVE-2026-34835 did not fully sanitize the Host header. Specifically, `host_with_port` could still return an invalid, raw Host header if `split_authority` returned an empty array, leading to an incomplete URL construction in `base_url`.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious Host header that, when used in `base_url` or `url`, could lead to an incomplete or malformed URL. This could potentially be used in scenarios like cache poisoning, SSRF, or other header injection attacks if the generated URL is used in subsequent requests or displayed to users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/rack/request.rb
+++ b/lib/rack/request.rb
@@ -320,8 +320,10 @@ def host_authority
       end
 
       def host_with_port(authority = self.authority)
-        host, _, port = split_authority(authority)
+        parts = split_authority(authority)
+        return nil if parts.empty?
 
+        host, _, port = parts
         if port == DEFAULT_PORTS[self.scheme]
           host
         else
@@ -591,7 +593,8 @@ def delete_param(k)
       end
 
       def base_url
-        "#{scheme}://#{host_with_port}"
+        hostport = host_with_port || host_with_port(server_authority)
+        "#{scheme}://#{hostport}"
       end
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check in `host_with_port` to return `nil` if `split_authority` yields an empty array, preventing the use of an invalid host. Additionally, `base_url` now falls back to `host_with_port(server_authority)` if the initial `host_with_port` returns `nil`, ensuring a valid host is always used for URL construction.

## GHSA-6f4g-xm8v-pgv6 · compose-lint

2026-11-10 · Python · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application printed user-controlled text from Compose files directly into GitHub Actions workflow logs without properly sanitizing characters that GitHub Actions interprets as workflow commands (e.g., `##[` and `::`). This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary workflow commands.

### Impact

An attacker could inject GitHub Actions workflow commands, potentially leading to arbitrary code execution within the CI/CD pipeline, information disclosure, or other malicious actions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/compose_lint/_output.py
+++ b/src/compose_lint/_output.py
@@ -76,13 +76,29 @@ def _escape(match: re.Match[str]) -> str:
     return f"\U{code:08x}" if code > 0xFFFF else f"\u{code:04x}"
 
 
+# CI log parsers treat some printable text as commands. GitHub Actions and
+# Azure Pipelines run `##[command]` found anywhere in a line, and GitHub also
+# runs `::command::` at the start of a line, after any indentation, which is
+# where `emit` puts a diagnostic's continuation lines. A Compose file's own
+# strings are printed in the report, so each opening is broken with the same
+# visible escape the control characters get.
+_LEGACY_COMMAND = re.compile(r"##\[")
+_LINE_COMMAND = re.compile(r"^([ \t]*):(?=:)", re.MULTILINE)
+
+
def _defuse_commands(text: str) -> str:
+    text = _LEGACY_COMMAND.sub("##\\u005b", text)
+    return _LINE_COMMAND.sub("\\1\\u003a", text)
+
+
def sanitize(text: str) -> str:
     """Render terminal-unsafe code points as visible ``\uXXXX`` escapes.
 
     Newlines and tabs survive, so multi-line content (fix guidance, a unified
-    diff) keeps its layout. Clean text is returned unchanged.
+    diff) keeps its layout. Clean text is returned unchanged. CI workflow
+    command openers (``##[`` and a line-leading ``::``) are escaped too.
     """
-    return _UNSAFE_OUTPUT_CHARS.sub(_escape, text)
+    return _defuse_commands(_UNSAFE_OUTPUT_CHARS.sub(_escape, text))
 
 
 def sanitize_line(text: str) -> str:
@@ -94,4 +110,4 @@ def sanitize_line(text: str) -> str:
     which is how a service name could forge a finding against another file or a
     ``✓ PASS`` verdict.
     """
-    return _UNSAFE_LINE_CHARS.sub(_escape, text)
+    return _defuse_commands(_UNSAFE_LINE_CHARS.sub(_escape, text))
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function `_defuse_commands` that specifically targets and escapes GitHub Actions workflow command openers (`##[` and line-leading `::`). This function is now applied to all user-controlled text before it is printed to the output, preventing command injection.

## GHSA-wrr5-j383-f7h2 · @nocobase/plugin-action-custom-request

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application allowed users to provide runtime options for custom HTTP requests, including sensitive target-related configurations like 'url', 'baseURL', and 'proxy'. This lack of validation meant an attacker could override the intended request target.

### Impact

An attacker could force the server to make requests to arbitrary internal or external URLs, potentially accessing internal network resources, cloud metadata, or other sensitive services.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/plugins/@nocobase/plugin-action-custom-request/src/server/actions/send.ts
+++ b/packages/plugins/@nocobase/plugin-action-custom-request/src/server/actions/send.ts
@@ -161,6 +179,14 @@ export async function send(this: CustomRequestPlugin, ctx: Context, next: Next)
     ctx.throw(404, 'request config not found');
   }
 
+  const forbiddenRuntimeOptionKeys = getForbiddenRuntimeTargetOptionKeys(runtimeOptions);
+  if (forbiddenRuntimeOptionKeys.length) {
+    return ctx.throw(
+      400,
+      ctx.t('Runtime request options cannot override the request target', { ns: 'action-custom-request' }),
+    );
+  }
+
   ctx.withoutDataWrapping = true;
 
   const mergedOptions = {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function `getForbiddenRuntimeTargetOptionKeys` which identifies if any sensitive target-related options (like 'url', 'baseURL', 'proxy') are present in the user-provided runtime options. If such options are found, the request is rejected with a 400 error.

## GHSA-2vgq-wchc-25w7 · node-forge

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **LOW** 3.7 · Pattern: `WEAK_CRYPTO→CIPHER`

### Root cause

The original implementation of PKCS#7 padding validation in node-forge only checked the last byte for the padding count and ensured it was within a valid range. It did not verify that all padding bytes actually matched the indicated padding count, allowing for malformed padding to be accepted.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially craft ciphertext that, when decrypted, could lead to padding oracle attacks or other cryptographic vulnerabilities, potentially allowing for plaintext recovery or message manipulation in specific scenarios.

### Key diff

```
-  if(count > (this.blockSize << 2)) {
+  if(count === 0 || count > this.blockSize || count > len) {
     return false;
   }
 
+  for(var i = 2; i <= count; ++i) {
+    if(output.at(len - i) !== count) {
+      return false;
+    }
+  }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a loop to explicitly check that all padding bytes, from the second-to-last byte up to the `count`-th byte from the end, match the `count` value. It also adds checks for `count === 0` and `count > len` to prevent issues with empty or malformed input.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 2 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, JavaScript
> → GHSA-8ffj-4hx4-9pgf

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/digitalbazaar/forge/commit/81774ff99e2da64aec9a43591df45a360040c136)

## GHSA-9wg5-mj3p-v49q · node-forge

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **LOW** 3.7 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the certificate signature verification logic incorrectly used the issuer's public key with the child certificate's signature algorithm (padding scheme). This mismatch could lead to a scenario where a specially crafted certificate with a different signature scheme could be accepted as valid.

### Impact

An attacker could forge a certificate that appears to be signed by a trusted issuer, potentially leading to impersonation or other security bypasses in systems that rely on certificate validation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/x509.js
+++ b/lib/x509.js
@@ -1186,8 +1188,12 @@ pki.createCertificate = function() {
     if(md !== null) {
+      // the padding scheme comes from the child's signature algorithm
       rval = _verifySignature({
-        certificate: cert, md: md, signature: child.signature
+        certificate: child,
+        publicKey: cert.publicKey,
+        md: md,
+        signature: child.signature
       });
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `_verifySignature` function to explicitly pass the `publicKey` to be used for verification, defaulting to the certificate's public key if not provided. In the `pki.createCertificate` function, when verifying a child certificate against its issuer, the issuer's public key is now correctly passed to `_verifySignature`, while the child's certificate is used to derive the signature algorithm (padding scheme).

## GHSA-p8x9-v4hv-2w4g · rack

2026-11-10 · Ruby · **LOW** 3.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The original regular expression to detect multiple boundary parameters in the Content-Type header was too narrow. It only checked the post_match of the first 'boundary=' occurrence, allowing an attacker to include a quoted or otherwise malformed first boundary parameter to bypass the guard, followed by a second, legitimate boundary parameter.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a Content-Type header that would bypass Web Application Firewalls (WAFs) or other security controls that relied on Rack's boundary ambiguity guard, potentially leading to the exploitation of other vulnerabilities that the WAF was intended to prevent.

### Key diff

```
-        if data.post_match.match?(/boundary\s*=/i)
+        if content_type.scan(/boundary(?:\*\d*\*?)?\s*=/i).size > 1
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the regular expression to scan the entire Content-Type header for all occurrences of 'boundary=' (including RFC 2231 'boundary*' parameters). It now counts the total number of boundary parameters, ensuring that if more than one is present, an error is raised, regardless of their format or position.

## GHSA-65v8-q222-64xx · compose-lint

2026-11-10 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The `compose-lint` tool's `fix --apply` functionality could delete lines not explicitly selected by the user due to an inconsistency in how file content was read and parsed. Specifically, `read_text()` (used for checking) performed universal-newline translation, while `open(newline="")` (used for fixing) did not. This discrepancy, combined with PyYAML's handling of ambiguous line breaks (like lone \r, U+0085, U+2028, U+2029), meant that the line numbers reported by the `check` command could differ from what the `fix` command actually processed, leading to unintended modifications.

### Impact

An attacker, or even a legitimate user, could experience unintended data loss or modification of their `compose` files. This could include the deletion of critical security settings or other important configurations, leading to a denial of service, misconfiguration, or potentially other security vulnerabilities if the deleted line was a security control.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/compose_lint/parser.py
+++ b/src/compose_lint/parser.py
@@ -708,7 +709,14 @@ def load_compose(
     """
     filepath = Path(path)
     try:
-        content = filepath.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+        with filepath.open(encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
+            content = fh.read()
     except FileNotFoundError:
         raise
     except UnicodeDecodeError as e:
@@ -752,6 +760,24 @@ def loads(
     Raises:
         ComposeError: If the text is not valid YAML or not a valid Compose file.
     """
+    ambiguous = find_ambiguous_break(content)
+    if ambiguous is not None:
+        line, description = ambiguous
+        raise ComposeError(
+            f"Ambiguous line break on line {line}: {description}. "
+            "This is a line break to the YAML parser but not to editors, SARIF "
+            "viewers or CI annotations, so no reported line number would be "
+            "correct for both. Use LF or CRLF line endings and remove the "
+            "character."
+        )
```

### Fix summary

The patch standardizes file reading to use `open(encoding="utf-8", newline="")` for both checking and fixing, ensuring consistent parsing. Additionally, it introduces a new check that explicitly refuses to process files containing ambiguous line breaks (like lone \r, U+0085, U+2028, U+2029) that are interpreted differently by PyYAML and common text editors. This prevents the tool from operating on documents where line numbering cannot be consistently reported.

## GHSA-88f4-frh5-gmcr · tmatens/compose-lint

2026-11-10 · YAML · **LOW** 0.0 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The application would read and parse YAML files, including those symlinked outside the project directory. If a symlinked file contained sensitive information or credentials and had a YAML parsing error, the error message would quote the problematic part of the file, leading to the disclosure of that sensitive data in logs or reports.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious symlink to a sensitive file outside the project, trigger a YAML parsing error, and cause the application to disclose parts of the sensitive file's content in error messages.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/compose_lint/_selection.py
+++ b/src/compose_lint/_selection.py
@@ -186,8 +198,16 @@ def _plan_discovered(
             env_files=env_files,
             gaps=tuple(gaps),
         )
-    discovered = [name for name in COMPOSE_FILENAMES if (directory / name).is_file()]
-    selection = _pair_with_overrides(discovered, merge_overrides, notes)
+    discovered = []
+    for name in COMPOSE_FILENAMES:
+        candidate = directory / name
+        if not candidate.is_file():
+            continue
+        if _links_outside(candidate):
+            gaps.append((str(candidate), _OUTSIDE_NOT_LINTED))
+            continue
+        discovered.append(name)
+    selection = _pair_with_overrides(discovered, merge_overrides, notes, gaps)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check to prevent the application from following symlinks that point outside the project directory when discovering or processing Compose files. It also removes a custom YAML error description function, relying on a safer, pre-existing utility that avoids quoting source lines in error messages.

## GHSA-8vv3-6qpc-gpw6 · bitcoinjs-lib

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The Merkle root calculation in `bitcoinjs-lib` did not detect when a transaction list contained duplicate Merkle nodes. An attacker could craft a transaction list where a duplicate node would result in the same Merkle root as a valid list, allowing a forged list to be validated.

### Impact

An attacker could create a block with a manipulated list of transactions that would appear valid to the library, potentially leading to the acceptance of invalid or malicious transactions within a block.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/block.js
+++ b/src/block.js
@@ -176,8 +180,13 @@ class Block {
   }
   __checkMerkleRoot() {
     if (!this.transactions) throw errorMerkleNoTxes;
-    const actualMerkleRoot = Block.calculateMerkleRoot(this.transactions);
-    return this.merkleRoot.compare(actualMerkleRoot) === 0;
+    const mutated = { value: false };
+    const actualMerkleRoot = Block.calculateMerkleRoot(
+      this.transactions,
+      false,
+      mutated,
+    );
+    return !mutated.value && this.merkleRoot.compare(actualMerkleRoot) === 0;
   }
```

### Fix summary

A new `mutated` parameter was added to `calculateMerkleRoot` and `fastMerkleRoot` functions. This parameter is a boolean flag that is set to true if any two adjacent Merkle tree nodes are found to be identical during the hash calculation, indicating a potential manipulation. The `__checkMerkleRoot` function now verifies that the Merkle root matches AND that no mutation (duplication) occurred.

## GHSA-chp3-j5qr-2vfr · effect

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The application did not sufficiently validate or sanitize user-supplied input for cookie names, domains, and paths before using them to construct the 'Set-Cookie' header. This allowed special characters to be injected, leading to the manipulation of cookie attributes.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary cookie attributes, potentially overwriting existing cookies, setting additional cookies, or manipulating security attributes like 'HttpOnly' or 'Secure', which could lead to session hijacking or other client-side attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/effect/src/unstable/http/Cookies.ts
+++ b/packages/effect/src/unstable/http/Cookies.ts
@@ -426,6 +426,10 @@ export const isEmpty = (self: Cookies): boolean => Record.isEmptyRecord(self.coo
 
 // oxlint-disable-next-line no-control-regex
 const fieldContentRegExp = /^[	 -~-ÿ]+$/
+const cookieNameRegExp = /^[!#$%&'*+\-.^_`|~0-9A-Za-z]+$/
+// oxlint-disable-next-line no-control-regex
+const cookieDomainRegExp = /^[\u0009\u0020-\u003a\u003c-\u007e\u0080-\u00ff]+$/
+const cookiePathRegExp = /^[\u0020-\u003a\u003c-\u007e]+$/
 
 const CookieProto = {
   [CookieTypeId]: CookieTypeId,
@@ -455,26 +459,10 @@ export function makeCookie(
   value: string,
   options?: Cookie["options"] | undefined
 ): Result.Result<Cookie, CookiesError> {
-  if (!fieldContentRegExp.test(name)) {
-    return Result.fail(CookiesError.fromReason("InvalidCookieName"))
-  }
   const encodedValue = encodeURIComponent(value)
-  if (encodedValue && !fieldContentRegExp.test(encodedValue)) {
-    return Result.fail(CookiesError.fromReason("InvalidCookieValue"))
-  }
-
-  if (options !== undefined) {
-    if (options.domain !== undefined && !fieldContentRegExp.test(options.domain)) {
-      return Result.fail(CookiesError.fromReason("InvalidCookieDomain"))
-    }
-
-    if (options.path !== undefined && !fieldContentRegExp.test(options.path)) {
-      return Result.fail(CookiesError.fromReason("InvalidCookiePath"))
-    }
-
-    if (options.maxAge !== undefined && !Duration.isFinite(Duration.fromInputUnsafe(options.maxAge))) {
-      return Result.fail(CookiesError.fromReason("CookieInfinityMaxAge"))
-    }
+  const error = validateCookie(name, encodedValue, options)
+  if (error !== undefined) {
+    return Result.fail(error)
   }
 
   return Result.succeed(Object.assign(Object.create(CookieProto), {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces more restrictive regular expressions for validating cookie names, domains, and paths. A new `validateCookie` function centralizes these checks, ensuring that only valid characters are allowed in these fields before the cookie is created or serialized.

## GHSA-jf3h-8jvx-vcrg · tmatens/compose-lint

2026-11-10 · YAML · **LOW** 0.0 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The `compose-lint` tool would directly quote substituted environment variable values, including secrets, into its findings messages and fix suggestions. These findings could then be logged or uploaded to external services like Code Scanning, exposing sensitive information.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a pull request that causes `compose-lint` to include a secret from a CI-generated `.env` file directly in its output, leading to the secret being exposed in CI logs, JSON reports, or SARIF files.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/compose_lint/engine.py
+++ b/src/compose_lint/engine.py
@@ -154,6 +165,7 @@ def run_rules(
                 findings.sort(key=lambda f: (f.line is None, f.line or 0))
                 raise FindingLimitError(findings)
 
+    if env_values:
+        findings = _quote_references(findings, services, env_values)
     findings.sort(key=lambda f: (f.line is None, f.line or 0))
     return findings
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a mechanism to replace actual environment variable values in findings with their `${"NAME"}` references. It identifies which environment variables were used by a service and, if the value is longer than a defined threshold, substitutes the value with its variable name in the finding's message and fix suggestion.

## GHSA-r7j4-crjv-h467 · compose-lint

2026-11-10 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `compose-lint` tool's output, specifically error messages and help text generated by `argparse`, was not properly sanitized. This allowed malicious text from user-controlled input (like file paths or environment variable names) to be interpreted as GitHub Actions workflow commands when the output was printed to `stdout` or `stderr` in a CI environment.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary GitHub Actions workflow commands, potentially leading to execution of malicious code, exfiltration of secrets, or other unauthorized actions within the CI pipeline.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/compose_lint/cli.py
+++ b/src/compose_lint/cli.py
@@ -15,11 +15,11 @@
 import traceback
 from dataclasses import replace
 from pathlib import Path
-from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, NoReturn
+from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, NoReturn, cast
 
 from compose_lint import __version__
 from compose_lint._env_file import ENV_FILENAME
-from compose_lint._output import emit, emit_block
+from compose_lint._output import defuse_json, emit, emit_block, sanitize
 from compose_lint._selection import Selection, plan_documents
 from compose_lint._service_env import Unread, describe_unread, resolve_env_files
 from compose_lint.config import (
@@ -509,9 +511,41 @@ def _add_init_subparser(
     )
 
 
+class _Parser(argparse.ArgumentParser):
+    """``argparse`` with its own output routed through the sanitizer.
+
+    An argument the parser rejects is echoed in its error (``unrecognized
+    arguments: …``), and a path from the repository reaches argv whenever a
+    harness appends file names without a ``--`` first. argparse writes that
+    straight to stderr, the one print site that bypassed :func:`emit`, so a
+    directory named ``--##[warning]…`` issued a workflow command. Subparsers
+    inherit the class, so every level is covered.
+    """
+
+    def print_usage(self, file: SupportsWrite[str] | None = None) -> None:
+        self._emit(self.format_usage(), file)
+
+    def print_help(self, file: SupportsWrite[str] | None = None) -> None:
+        self._emit(self.format_help(), file)
+
+    def exit(self, status: int = 0, message: str | None = None) -> NoReturn:
+        if message:
+            emit_block(message)
+        sys.exit(status)
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def _emit(text: str, file: SupportsWrite[str] | None) -> None:
+        # argparse's own default: help and usage go to stdout unless the caller
+        # (an error) asked for stderr.
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an `inline` function to sanitize strings before they are embedded into output messages, preventing them from being interpreted as workflow commands. It also customizes `argparse.ArgumentParser` to route its output (usage, help, errors) through a sanitizer, ensuring all user-controlled input displayed by the parser is safe.

## GHSA-rm8w-w798-g545 · tmatens/compose-lint

2026-11-10 · YAML · **LOW** 0.0 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The previous fix for GHSA-jf3h-8jvx-vcrg did not fully address the issue of sensitive information being quoted in error messages. When the finding limit was exceeded, the `_quote_references` function, which redacts sensitive values, was not called before the `FindingLimitError` was raised, leading to secrets from CI-generated `.env` files being exposed in the error message.

### Impact

An attacker or unauthorized user could potentially gain access to sensitive information, such as API keys or other secrets, if the `compose-lint` tool processed a document that exceeded the finding limit and the error message was logged or displayed.

### Key diff

```
if len(findings) > MAX_FINDINGS:
+                if env_values:
+                    findings = _quote_references(findings, services, env_values)
                 findings.sort(key=lambda f: (f.line is None, f.line or 0))
                 raise FindingLimitError(findings)
```

### Fix summary

The patch ensures that the `_quote_references` function is called to redact sensitive values from findings before a `FindingLimitError` is raised due to exceeding the maximum finding limit. This prevents secrets from being included in the error message.

## GHSA-xqc2-xf4c-x3h3 · react-server-dom-webpack

2026-11-10 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an improper handling of ArrayBuffer views in React Server Components, particularly when dealing with byte streams that detach ArrayBuffers. An attacker could craft a malicious payload that, when processed by the server, would lead to an ArrayBuffer being detached prematurely or unexpectedly, allowing for arbitrary code execution.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could achieve remote code execution on the server running React Server Components, potentially leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/react-server/src/ReactServerStreamConfigNode.js
+++ b/packages/react-server/src/ReactServerStreamConfigNode.js
@@ -103,6 +109,27 @@ function writeViewChunk(
   chunk: PrecomputedChunk | BinaryChunk,
 ) {
   if (chunk.byteLength === 0) {
+    if (supportsArrayBufferDetached) {
+      // $FlowFixMe[prop-missing] -- supportsArrayBufferDetached implies it does exist.
+      if (chunk.buffer.detached) {
+        throw new Error(
+          'Cannot transport a detached buffer. ' +
+            'This usually happens when trying to transport multiple buffer-spanning views on the same underyling buffer. ' +
+            'Either reuse the exact same view by identity or copy the underlying buffer manually via `view.slice()`.',
+        );
+      }
+    } else if (__DEV__) {
+      if (!didWarnAboutMissingArrayBufferDetached) {
+        console.error(
+          'React encountered an empty buffer when writing in an environment that ' +
+            'does not support ArrayBuffer.prototype.detached. ' +
+            'This could be a security issue if the buffer was not actually empty. ' +
+            'Please upgrade to a Node.js version that supports ArrayBuffer.prototype.detached.',
+        );
+        didWarnAboutMissingArrayBufferDetached = true;
+      }
+    }
+
     return;
   }
   if (chunk.byteLength > VIEW_SIZE) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces checks for detached ArrayBuffers and defensively slices views into new buffers when necessary, especially for chunks larger than a certain size or in environments lacking `ArrayBuffer.prototype.detached`. It also refactors the `BackingFormData` to prevent quadratic lookup times and improve handling of various entry types.
